It’s been happening enough that I figured I should come in and talk about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble with tasks that used to be routine for you, like managing bills, following recipes, or keeping track of events?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much the kind of thing I mean. I’ll forget a heart follow-up appointment, or I’ll be supposed to call the clinic back and it just slips my mind. And with repair jobs, I’ll show up and then realize I didn’t bring the right tools. It’s not every single day, but it’s happened enough that it’s become a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to remember recent events or learn new information, like names, conversations, or instructions?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the practical stuff I’m noticing. I’ll forget a heart follow-up appointment, or that I was supposed to call the clinic back. And sometimes I’ll go out to do a repair job and realize I didn’t bring the right tools. That’s the kind of recent thing that keeps slipping.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when you had trouble remembering something new, like a conversation you had earlier the same day or instructions someone just gave you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like if I’m at the bridge table, or at a Fix-It Friends planning meeting, I start out trying to really listen. But after a little while my mind kind of drifts, and then I realize I missed a good part of what someone was saying. So it’s not that I can’t hear them, I just don’t stay with it the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to stay focused on tasks or conversations, or do you feel overwhelmed when you need to plan or organize something?\n\nPatient: Yes, staying focused is definitely hard sometimes. I can sit down at the bridge table or go into one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings fully meaning to pay attention, but after a bit my mind just sort of slips away. Then I come back to it and realize I missed half of what was being said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you’re having trouble remembering things that you used to have no problem with, like names of people you’ve met before or how to get to places you’ve been to several times?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in that way. I haven’t really noticed getting lost going places I know, or forgetting people I already know. It’s more that I’m not always sure I took in the information in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it difficult to plan or organize tasks, like setting up a repair job or managing your schedule, even when you're not forgetting anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t noticed a big problem with planning a repair job or keeping my schedule straight. If something goes wrong, it’s usually more that I didn’t catch all the details when someone explained it, not that I can’t organize it once I know what needs doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt confused about the day, month, or year, or found yourself unsure of where you are or how you got there?\n\nPatient: No, I can’t say I’ve had that happen. I know what day it is and where I am. I don’t find myself somewhere wondering how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, discouraged, or uninterested in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I mean, I have my off days like anyone, but I haven’t noticed being especially down or losing interest in things I usually like.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble recognizing objects or people you see regularly, like forgetting what a wrench is used for or not recognizing a neighbor you know well?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I recognize people I know, and I know what everyday tools and things are for. I don’t find myself somewhere wondering how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, discouraged, or uninterested in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I haven’t had trouble like that.\n\nDoctor: If someone gives you a short list of things to remember, like three items or a simple message, can you still recall them after a few minutes if you're not reminded?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t paid close attention to that. I think with a short list I usually do okay, but if I’m distracted, I might need it repeated.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not every single day, but it’s happened enough that it’s become a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to remember recent events or learn new information, like names, conversations, or instructions?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the practical stuff I’m noticing. I’ll forget a heart follow-up appointment, or that I was supposed to call the clinic back. And sometimes I’ll go out to do a repair job and realize I didn’t bring the right tools. That’s the kind of recent thing that keeps slipping.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when you had trouble remembering something new, like a conversation you had earlier the same day or instructions someone just gave you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like if I’m at the bridge table, or at a Fix-It Friends planning meeting, I start out trying to really listen. But after a little while my mind kind of drifts, and then I realize I missed a good part of what someone was saying. So it’s not that I can’t hear them, I just don’t stay with it the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it hard to stay focused on tasks or conversations, or do you feel overwhelmed when you need to plan or organize something?\n\nPatient: Yes, staying focused is definitely hard sometimes. I can sit down at the bridge table or go into one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings fully meaning to pay attention, but after a bit my mind just sort of slips away. Then I come back to it and realize I missed half of what was being said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you’re having trouble remembering things that you used to have no problem with, like names of people you’ve met before or how to get to places you’ve been to several times?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in that way. I haven’t really noticed getting lost going places I know, or forgetting people I already know. It’s more that I’m not always sure I took in the information in the first place.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it difficult to plan or organize tasks, like setting up a repair job or managing your schedule, even when you're not forgetting anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I haven’t noticed a big problem with planning a repair job or keeping my schedule straight. If something goes wrong, it’s usually more that I didn’t catch all the details when someone explained it, not that I can’t organize it once I know what needs doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt confused about the day, month, or year, or found yourself unsure of where you are or how you got there?\n\nPatient: No, I can’t say I’ve had that happen. I know what day it is and where I am. I don’t find myself somewhere wondering how I got there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, discouraged, or uninterested in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really sure. I mean, I have my off days like anyone, but I haven’t noticed being especially down or losing interest in things I usually like.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble recognizing objects or people you see regularly, like forgetting what a wrench is used for or not recognizing a neighbor you know well?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I recognize people I know, and I know what everyday tools and things are for. I haven’t had trouble like that.\n\nDoctor: If someone gives you a short list of things to remember, like three items or a simple message, can you still recall them after a few minutes if you're not reminded?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t paid close attention to that. I think with a short list I usually do okay, but if I’m distracted, I might need it repeated.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods where you felt persistently down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I’ve had days where I feel low or not much like doing things, but not most of the day every day for a couple weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble handling more complex tasks, like managing your finances, following a detailed repair plan, or organizing a project, even when you're not forgetting anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really handle many big projects these days. With bills or anything detailed, I can usually get through it if I take my time, but I haven’t noticed a major problem beyond just needing to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate, like being easily distracted or having trouble staying focused even when you're trying your best?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have noticed that. I’ll sit down at the bridge table, or at one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I mean to listen closely. But after a little while my mind kind of drifts off, and then I realize I missed a good chunk of what people were saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any worsening in your ability to remember important information or complete familiar tasks, even when you're not distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s really what brought me in. I keep forgetting things I shouldn’t be forgetting, like heart follow-up appointments, or that I’m supposed to call the clinic back. And sometimes I’ll go to help with a repair job and realize I didn’t bring the right tools. It’s familiar stuff, so it worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble making decisions or solving problems that used to be routine for you, like figuring out how to fix something or handling everyday financial matters?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a big problem with making decisions or figuring things out once I’m actually focused on it. I might take a little longer sometimes, but I can usually work through routine stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t really handle many big projects these days. With bills or anything detailed, I can usually get through it if I take my time, but I haven’t noticed a major problem beyond just needing to focus on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate, like being easily distracted or having trouble staying focused even when you're trying your best?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have noticed that. I’ll sit down at the bridge table, or at one of those Fix-It Friends planning meetings, and I mean to listen closely. But after a little while my mind kind of drifts off, and then I realize I missed a good chunk of what people were saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any worsening in your ability to remember important information or complete familiar tasks, even when you're not distracted or stressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s really what brought me in. I keep forgetting things I shouldn’t be forgetting, like heart follow-up appointments, or that I’m supposed to call the clinic back. And sometimes I’ll go to help with a repair job and realize I didn’t bring the right tools. It’s familiar stuff, so it worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble making decisions or solving problems that used to be routine for you, like figuring out how to fix something or handling everyday financial matters?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a big problem with making decisions or figuring things out once I’m actually focused on it. I might take a little longer sometimes, but I can usually work through routine stuff.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall information later, like what someone just told you, even when you weren’t distracted at the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes I can remember what someone told me, but I haven’t paid close enough attention to whether it happens even when I’m not distracted. It’s hard for me to separate the two.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems getting along with others or handling responsibilities at work or home that weren’t an issue before?\n\nPatient: Yes, at least with responsibilities. I’m not working anymore, but with the senior-center repair group, they can’t really count on me like they used to. And my doctor’s office has had to call and chase me because I missed heart follow-ups. That didn’t used to be such a problem."
